.

G_{422}^{I}\times G_{422}^{II}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\,.

(517)

The new
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
discrete symmetry is introduced for phenomenological
purposes, as it will prevent fine-tuning, reduce the total number
of parameters of the model and protect from FCNCs involving the first
family of SM-like chiral fermions. Moreover,
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
will simplify
the diagonalisation of the full mass matrices and preserve the success
of the effective Yukawa couplings for SM fermions given in Section
20.3
,
with specific modifications. The origin of the chiral fermion families
is still the second Pati-Salam group, however now they transform in
a non-trivial way under
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
,

\psi_{1,2,3}(\mathbf{1,1,1;4,2,1})_{(\alpha,1,1)}\,,\quad\psi_{1,2,3}^{c}(\mathbf{1,1,1;\overline{4},1,\overline{2}})_{(\alpha,\alpha^{2},1)}\,.

(518)

Finally, the scalar content is extended by an additional scalar
\Omega_{15}

which transforms in the adjoint representation of
SU(4)^{I}_{PS}
, whose
VEV
\left\langle\Omega_{15}\right\rangle=T_{15}^{I}v_{15}
splits
the vector-like masses of quarks and leptons, where
T^{I}_{15}=\mathrm{diag}(1,1,1,-3)/(2\sqrt{6})\,.

We also include an additional copy of the Yukon
\overline{\phi}
,
denoted as
\overline{\phi^{\prime}}
, featuring
\alpha^{2}
charge under

\mathbb{Z}_{4}
. The simplified Lagrangian in Eq. (
424
)
is extended by the new matter content to

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{mass}}^{ren}&=y_{ia}^{\psi}\overline{H}\psi_{i}\psi_{a}^{c}+y_{a3}^{\psi}H\psi_{a}\psi_{3}^{c}+x_{ia}^{\psi}\phi\psi_{i}\overline{\psi}_{a}+x_{a2}^{\psi^{c}}\overline{\psi_{a}^{c}\phi^{\prime}}\psi_{2}^{c}+x_{a3}^{\psi^{c}}\overline{\psi_{a}^{c}\phi}\psi_{3}^{c}&{}\\
{}&+x_{16}^{\psi}\phi\psi_{1}\overline{\psi}_{6}+x_{61}^{\psi^{c}}\overline{\psi_{6}^{c}\phi}\psi_{1}^{c}+M_{ab}^{\psi}\psi_{a}\overline{\psi_{b}}+M_{ab}^{\psi^{c}}\psi_{a}^{c}\overline{\psi_{b}^{c}}+M_{66}^{\psi}\psi_{6}\overline{\psi_{6}}+M_{66}^{\psi^{c}}\psi_{6}^{c}\overline{\psi_{6}^{c}}&{}\\
{}&+\lambda_{15}^{aa}\Omega_{15}\psi_{a}\overline{\psi_{a}}+\lambda_{15}^{66}\Omega_{15}\psi_{6}\overline{\psi_{6}}+\bar{\lambda}_{15}^{aa}\Omega_{15}\psi_{a}^{c}\overline{\psi_{a}^{c}}+\bar{\lambda}_{15}^{66}\Omega_{15}\psi_{6}^{c}\overline{\psi_{6}^{c}}+\mathrm{h.c.}\,,&{}\end{aligned}

(519)

where
i=2,3
and
a,b=4,5
(terms
i=1
and
a,b=6
forbidden
by
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
). The symmetry breaking and the decomposition of the various
fields proceeds just like in the simplified model, see Section
20.2
,
however the VEVs of the additional scalars
\overline{\phi^{\prime}}
and

\Omega_{15}
play a role in the spontaneous breaking of the 4321
symmetry, and the corresponding gauge boson masses become (assuming

v_{1,3}\approx\overline{v}_{1,3}\approx\overline{v^{\prime}}_{1,3}
for
simplicity)

\displaystyle M_{U_{1}}=\frac{1}{2}g_{4}\sqrt{3v_{1}^{2}+3v_{3}^{2}+\frac{4}{3}v_{15}^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle M_{g^{\prime}}=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{g_{4}^{2}+g_{3}^{2}}v_{3}\,,

(520)

\displaystyle M_{Z^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{g_{4}^{2}+\frac{2}{3}g_{1}^{2}}\sqrt{3v_{1}^{2}+v_{3}^{2}}\,.

23.2
Effective Yukawa couplings in the extended model

In this section, we diagonalise the full mass matrix of the extended
model, following the same procedure as in Section
20.3
,
but including the extra matter content of the extended model. We may
write the mass terms and couplings in Eq. (
519
)
as a
9\times 9
matrix in flavour space (we also define 9-dimensional
vectors as
\psi_{\alpha}
and
\psi_{\beta}^{c}
below),

\mathcal{L}_{4,5,6}^{ren}=\psi_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{T}}M^{\psi}\psi_{\beta}^{c}+\mathrm{h.c.}\,,

(521)

\psi_{\alpha}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}\psi_{1}&\psi_{2}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\psi_{5}&\psi_{6}&\overline{\psi_{4}^{c}}&\overline{\psi_{5}^{c}}&\overline{\psi_{6}^{c}}\end{array}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\,,

(522)

\psi_{\beta}^{c}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}\psi_{1}^{c}&\psi_{2}^{c}&\psi_{3}^{c}&\psi_{4}^{c}&\psi_{5}^{c}&\psi_{6}^{c}&\overline{\psi_{4}}&\overline{\psi_{5}}&\overline{\psi_{6}}\end{array}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\,,

(523)

M^{\psi}=\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}llcccccccc@{}}&\lx@intercol\hfil\psi_{1}^{c}\hfil&\psi_{2}^{c}&\psi_{3}^{c}&\psi_{4}^{c}&\psi_{5}^{c}&\psi_{6}^{c}&\overline{\psi_{4}}&\overline{\psi_{5}}&\overline{\psi_{6}}\\
\cline{2-10}\cr\left.\psi_{1}\right|&0&0&0&0&0&0&0&0&x_{16}^{\psi}\phi\\
\left.\psi_{2}\right|&0&0&0&y_{24}^{\psi}\overline{H}&y_{25}^{\psi}\overline{H}&0&0&x_{25}^{\psi}\phi&0\\
\left.\psi_{3}\right|&0&0&0&y_{34}^{\psi}\overline{H}&y_{35}^{\psi}\overline{H}&0&x_{34}^{\psi}\phi&x_{35}^{\psi}\phi&0\\
\left.\psi_{4}\right|&0&0&y_{43}^{\psi}H&0&0&0&M_{44}^{Q,L}&M_{45}^{\psi}&0\\
\left.\psi_{5}\right|&0&0&y_{53}^{\psi}H&0&0&0&M_{54}^{\psi}&M_{55}^{Q,L}&0\\
\left.\psi_{6}\right|&0&0&0&0&0&0&0&0&M_{66}^{Q,L}\\
\left.\overline{\psi_{4}^{c}}\right|&0&x_{42}^{\psi^{c}}\overline{\phi^{\prime}}&x_{43}^{\psi^{c}}\overline{\phi}&M_{44}^{\psi^{c}}&M_{45}^{\psi^{c}}&0&0&0&0\\
\left.\overline{\psi_{5}^{c}}\right|&0&x_{52}^{\psi^{c}}\overline{\phi^{\prime}}&x_{53}^{\psi^{c}}\overline{\phi}&M_{54}^{\psi^{c}}&M_{55}^{\psi^{c}}&0&0&0&0\\
\left.\overline{\psi_{6}^{c}}\right|&x_{61}^{\psi^{c}}\overline{\phi}&0&0&0&0&M_{66}^{\psi^{c}}&0&0&0\end{array}\right)\,,

(524)

where the diagonal mass parameters
M_{44,55,66}^{Q,L}
are split
for quarks and leptons due to the VEV of
\Omega_{15}
,

M_{aa}^{Q}\equiv M_{aa}^{\psi}+\frac{\lambda_{15}^{aa}\left\langle\Omega_{15}\right\rangle}{2\sqrt{6}}\,,\qquad M_{aa}^{L}\equiv M_{aa}^{\psi}-3\frac{\lambda_{15}^{aa}\left\langle\Omega_{15}\right\rangle}{2\sqrt{6}}\,,

(525)

where
a=4,5,6
. Similar equations are obtained for the
\psi^{c}

sector, however in the
\psi^{c}
sector the mass splitting is minimal
due to
\left\langle\Omega_{15}\right\rangle
being of order a
few hundreds GeV while
M_{aa}^{\psi^{c}}
are much heavier due to
a generalisation of the messenger dominance in Eq. (
546
).
The zeros in Eq. (
524
) are enforced by the

\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry, except for the zero in the (2,7) entry which is
obtained by rotating
\psi_{2}
and
\psi_{3}
, without loss of
generality thanks to the zeros in the upper
3\times 3
block (see
Section
16.3
and the discussion therein).

The matrix in Eq. (
524
) features three different
mass scales, the Higgs VEVs
\left\langle H\right\rangle
and
\langle\overline{H}\rangle
,
the Yukon VEVs
\left\langle\phi\right\rangle
,
\langle\overline{\phi}\rangle
,

\langle\overline{\phi^{\prime}}\rangle
and the vector-like mass terms
M_{ab}^{\psi}

and
M_{ab}^{\psi^{c}}
. We can block diagonalise the matrix above
by taking advantage of the different mass scales. Firstly, we diagonalise
the
2\times 2
sub-blocks containing the heavy masses
M_{ab}^{\psi}

and
M_{ab}^{\psi^{c}}
,

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}M_{4}^{Q}&0\\
0&M_{5}^{Q}\end{array}\right)=V_{45}^{Q}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}M_{44}^{Q}&M_{45}^{\psi}\\
M_{54}^{\psi}&M_{55}^{Q}\end{array}\right)V_{45}^{\overline{Q}\dagger}\,,

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}M_{4}^{L}&0\\
0&M_{5}^{L}\end{array}\right)=V_{45}^{L}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}M_{44}^{L}&M_{45}^{\psi}\\
M_{54}^{\psi}&M_{55}^{L}\end{array}\right)V_{45}^{\overline{L}\dagger}\,,

and similarly in the
\psi^{c}
sector. The 4-5 rotations above just
redefine the elements in the 4th, 5th, 7th and 8th rows and columns
of the full mass matrix, leaving the upper
3\times 3
blocks unchanged
(plus we are allowed to introduce now the zero in the (2,7) entry
by performing the rotation of
\psi_{2}
and
\psi_{3}
). Then we
perform a further sequence of rotations to go to the decoupling basis,
where no large elements appear apart from the diagonal heavy masses
(i.e. those terms in the seventh, eighth and ninth rows and columns
involving the fields
\phi
and
\overline{\phi}
are all absorbed
into a redefinition of the heavy masses), and we obtain a block-diagonal
matrix similar to that of Eq. (
439
)
but enlarged with the fifth and sixth vector-like families. The total set of
unitary transformations is given by

\begin{array}[]{c}V_{\psi}=V_{16}^{\psi}V_{35}^{\psi}V_{25}^{\psi}V_{34}^{\psi}V_{45}^{\psi}V_{45}^{\overline{\psi^{c}}}\,,\end{array}

(534)

V_{\psi^{c}}=V_{16}^{\psi^{c}}V_{35}^{\psi^{c}}V_{25}^{\psi^{c}}V_{34}^{\psi^{c}}V_{24}^{\psi^{c}}V_{45}^{\psi^{c}}V_{45}^{\overline{\psi}}\approx V_{34}^{\psi^{c}}V_{24}^{\psi^{c}}\,.

(535)

The mixing angles controlling the unitary transformations can be obtained
in the large mixing angle formalism (see Section
20.3
)
as

\displaystyle s_{34}^{Q}=\frac{x_{34}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{34}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{4}^{Q}\right)^{2}}}\,,

\displaystyle s_{34}^{L}=\frac{x_{34}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{34}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{4}^{L}\right)^{2}}}\,,

(536)

\displaystyle s_{25}^{Q}=\frac{x_{25}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{25}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{5}^{Q}\right)^{2}}}\,,

\displaystyle s_{25}^{L}=\frac{x_{25}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{25}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{5}^{L}\right)^{2}}}\,,

(537)

\displaystyle s_{35}^{Q}=\frac{c_{34}^{Q}x_{35}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(c_{34}^{Q}x_{35}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(x_{25}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{5}^{Q}\right)^{2}}},

\displaystyle s_{35}^{L}=\frac{c_{34}^{L}x_{35}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(c_{34}^{L}x_{35}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(x_{25}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{5}^{L}\right)^{2}}}\,,

(538)

\displaystyle s_{16}^{Q}=\frac{x_{16}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{16}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{6}^{Q}\right)^{2}}}\,,

\displaystyle s_{16}^{L}=\frac{x_{16}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{16}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{6}^{L}\right)^{2}}}\,,

(539)

\displaystyle s_{24}^{q^{c}}=\frac{x_{42}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{3}}\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{42}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{3}}\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{4}^{\psi^{c}}\right)^{2}}}\,,

\displaystyle s_{24}^{e^{c}}=\frac{x_{42}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{1}}\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{42}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{1}}\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{4}^{\psi^{c}}\right)^{2}}}\,,

(540)

\displaystyle s_{34}^{q^{c}}=\frac{x_{43}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{3}}\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{42}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{3}}\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(x_{43}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{3}}\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{4}^{\psi^{c}}\right)^{2}}}\,,

\displaystyle s_{34}^{e^{c}}=\frac{x_{43}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi}_{1}\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{42}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{1}}\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(x_{43}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{1}}\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{4}^{\psi^{c}}\right)^{2}}}\,,

(541)

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{4}^{Q}=\sqrt{\left(x_{34}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{4}^{Q}\right)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{4}^{L}=\sqrt{\left(x_{34}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{4}^{L}\right)^{2}}\,,

(542)

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{5}^{Q}=\sqrt{\left(x_{25}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(x_{35}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{5}^{Q}\right)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{5}^{L}=\sqrt{\left(x_{25}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(x_{35}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{5}^{L}\right)^{2}}\,,

(543)

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{6}^{Q}=\sqrt{\left(x_{16}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{6}^{Q}\right)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{6}^{L}=\sqrt{\left(x_{16}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{6}^{L}\right)^{2}}\,,

(544)

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{4}^{q^{c}}=\sqrt{\left(x_{42}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{3}}\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(x_{43}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{3}}\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{4}^{\psi^{c}}\right)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{4}^{e^{c}}=\sqrt{\left(x_{42}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{1}}\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(x_{43}^{\psi^{c}}\langle\overline{\phi_{1}}\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{4}^{\psi^{c}}\right)^{2}}\,.

(545)

The transformations in the
\psi^{c}
sector shown in Eq. (
535
)
can be described by
V_{34}^{\psi^{c}}V_{24}^{\psi^{c}}
in good
approximation, whose mixing angles are given above. This approximation
is accurate as far as the mixing involving the 5th and 6th
\psi^{c}

fields is further suppressed by a generalisation of the messenger dominance
in Eq. (
458
) to three vector-like families,
namely

M_{44}^{Q,L}\ll M_{55}^{Q,L}\sim M_{66}^{Q,L}\ll M_{44}^{\psi^{c}}\ll M_{55}^{\psi^{c}},M_{66}^{\psi^{c}}\,.

(546)

The hierarchy above will preserve most features of the simplified
model, including large third family Yukawa couplings arising from mixing
with
\psi_{4}
fermions, and small second family Yukawa couplings
arising from mixing with
\psi_{4}^{c}
fermions. The couplings of

U_{1}
to chiral fermions will remain dominantly left-handed, since
the couplings to
\psi^{c}
chiral fermions (or equivalently right-handed
fermions) will remain suppressed by small mixing angles. On the other
hand, the hierarchy
M_{44}^{Q,L}\ll M_{55}^{Q,L}
will provide hierarchical
couplings of
U_{1}
to third family and second family fermions,
so we anticipate a small contribution to
R_{K^{(*)}}
and a large
contribution to
R_{D^{(*)}}
.

We obtain the effective Yukawa couplings for SM fermions by applying
the set of unitary transformations in Eqs. (
534
)
and (
535
) to the upper
6\times 6
block
of Eq. (
524
), in the same way as in Eq. (
455
).
In this basis (primed), the mass matrix for each charged sector reads
(assuming a small
x_{35}^{\psi}
, see Section
24.3

for the motivation, and approximating cosines in the
\psi^{c}
sector
to be 1),

M_{\mathrm{eff}}^{u}=\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}llcc@{}}&\lx@intercol\hfil\phantom{\!\,}u^{\prime}{}_{1}^{c}\hfil&\phantom{\!\,}u^{\prime}{}_{2}^{c}&\phantom{\!\,}u^{\prime}{}_{3}^{c}\\
\cline{2-4}\cr\left.Q^{\prime}_{1}\right|&0&0&0\\
\left.Q^{\prime}_{2}\right|&0&0&s_{25}^{Q}y_{53}^{\psi}\\
\left.Q^{\prime}_{3}\right|&0&0&s_{34}^{Q}y_{43}^{\psi}\end{array}\right)\left\langle H_{t}\right\rangle+\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}llcc@{}}&\lx@intercol\hfil\phantom{\!\,}u^{\prime}{}_{1}^{c}\hfil&\phantom{\!\,}u^{\prime}{}_{2}^{c}&\phantom{\!\,}u^{\prime}{}_{3}^{c}\\
\cline{2-4}\cr\left.Q^{\prime}_{1}\right|&0&0&0\\
\left.Q^{\prime}_{2}\right|&0&c_{25}^{Q}s_{24}^{q^{c}}y_{24}^{\psi}&c_{25}^{Q}s_{34}^{q^{c}}y_{24}^{\psi}\\
\left.Q^{\prime}_{3}\right|&0&c_{34}^{Q}s_{24}^{q^{c}}y_{34}^{\psi}&c_{34}^{Q}s_{34}^{q^{c}}y_{34}^{\psi}\end{array}\right)\left\langle H_{c}\right\rangle+\mathrm{h.c.}\,,

(547)

M_{\mathrm{eff}}^{d}=\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}llcc@{}}&\lx@intercol\hfil\phantom{\!\,}d^{\prime}{}_{1}^{c}\hfil&\phantom{\!\,}d^{\prime}{}_{2}^{c}&\phantom{\!\,}d^{\prime}{}_{3}^{c}\\
\cline{2-4}\cr\left.Q^{\prime}_{1}\right|&0&0&0\\
\left.Q^{\prime}_{2}\right|&0&0&s_{25}^{Q}y_{53}^{\psi}\\
\left.Q^{\prime}_{3}\right|&0&0&s_{34}^{Q}y_{43}^{\psi}\end{array}\right)\left\langle H_{b}\right\rangle+\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}llcc@{}}&\lx@intercol\hfil\phantom{\!\,}d^{\prime}{}_{1}^{c}\hfil&\phantom{\!\,}d^{\prime}{}_{2}^{c}&\phantom{\!\,}d^{\prime}{}_{3}^{c}\\
\cline{2-4}\cr\left.Q^{\prime}_{1}\right|&0&0&0\\
\left.Q^{\prime}_{2}\right|&0&c_{25}^{Q}s_{24}^{q^{c}}y_{24}^{\psi}&c_{25}^{Q}s_{34}^{q^{c}}y_{24}^{\psi}\\
\left.Q^{\prime}_{3}\right|&0&c_{34}^{Q}s_{24}^{q^{c}}y_{34}^{\psi}&c_{34}^{Q}s_{34}^{q^{c}}y_{34}^{\psi}\end{array}\right)\left\langle H_{s}\right\rangle+\mathrm{h.c.}\,,

(548)

M_{\mathrm{eff}}^{e}=\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}llcc@{}}&\lx@intercol\hfil\phantom{\!\,}e^{\prime}{}_{1}^{c}\hfil&\phantom{\!\,}e^{\prime}{}_{2}^{c}&\phantom{\!\,}e^{\prime}{}_{3}^{c}\\
\cline{2-4}\cr\left.L^{\prime}_{1}\right|&0&0&0\\
\left.L^{\prime}_{2}\right|&0&0&s_{25}^{L}y_{53}^{\psi}\\
\left.L^{\prime}_{3}\right|&0&0&s_{34}^{L}y_{43}^{\psi}\end{array}\right)\left\langle H_{\tau}\right\rangle+\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}llcc@{}}&\lx@intercol\hfil\phantom{\!\,}e^{\prime}{}_{1}^{c}\hfil&\phantom{\!\,}e^{\prime}{}_{2}^{c}&\phantom{\!\,}e^{\prime}{}_{3}^{c}\\
\cline{2-4}\cr\left.L^{\prime}_{1}\right|&0&0&0\\
\left.L^{\prime}_{2}\right|&0&c_{25}^{L}s_{24}^{e^{c}}y_{24}^{\psi}&c_{25}^{L}s_{34}^{e^{c}}y_{24}^{\psi}\\
\left.L^{\prime}_{3}\right|&0&c_{34}^{L}s_{24}^{e^{c}}y_{34}^{\psi}&c_{34}^{L}s_{34}^{e^{c}}y_{34}^{\psi}\end{array}\right)\left\langle H_{\mu}\right\rangle+\mathrm{h.c.}\,,

(549)

which are diagonalised by 2-3 rotations, and the CKM matrix is obtained
via Eq. (
465
). The first family masses and mixings can be obtained by adding a heavier family of vector-like fermions that originates from the second PS group, as shown in Section
20.4
, however this has no implications for low-energy phenomenology. The mass matrices above are of similar
form to Eqs. (
459
), (
460
), (
461
),
just featuring an extra off-diagonal component in the (2,3) entry
of the first matrix in each sector, arising from mixing with the 5th
family. This new term can be used to partially cancel the down-quark 2-3
mixing while simultaneously enhancing the up-quark mixing to preserve the CKM,
involving a mild tuning:

•

Let us impose that the total (2,3) entry in the down-quark mass matrix
is small, i.e.

-s_{25}^{Q}\left|y_{53}^{\psi}\right|\left\langle H_{b}\right\rangle+c_{25}^{Q}s_{34}^{q^{c}}y_{24}^{\psi}\left\langle H_{s}\right\rangle\approx 0\,.

(550)

Following the discussion of Section
20.3
,
a natural benchmark is
\left\langle H_{b}\right\rangle\approx m_{b}

and
s_{34}^{q^{c}}y_{24}^{\psi}\left\langle H_{s}\right\rangle\approx m_{s}
,
hence

-s_{25}^{Q}\left|y_{53}^{\psi}\right|m_{b}+m_{s}\approx 0\Rightarrow\left|y_{53}^{\psi}\right|=\frac{m_{s}}{s_{25}^{Q}m_{b}}\,.

(551)

On the other hand, the mixing angle
s_{25}^{Q}
is very relevant
for the
B
-decays and related phenomenology, and we obtain the typical
value
s_{25}^{Q}\approx 0.2
in Section
24
,
featuring another connection between the flavour puzzle and
B
-physics
in our model. With this input, we obtain

\left|y_{53}^{\psi}\right|\approx\mathcal{O}(0.1).

(552)

In particular, the benchmark in Table
15
suppresses the
down mixing with the choice
y_{53}^{\psi}=-0.3
, obtaining
s_{23}^{d}\approx\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})

which is enough to control the stringent constraints from
B_{s}-\bar{B}_{s}

meson mixing (see Section
22.2
).

•

At the same time that
y_{53}^{\psi}
partially cancels the down
mixing, it leads to large up mixing which preserves the CKM. Let us
now estimate the 2-3 mixing in the up sector as the ratio of the (2,3)
entry over the (3,3) entry in the up-quark mass matrix,

\frac{-s_{25}^{Q}\left|y_{53}^{\psi}\right|\left\langle H_{t}\right\rangle+c_{25}^{Q}s_{34}^{q^{c}}y_{24}^{\psi}\left\langle H_{c}\right\rangle}{s_{34}^{Q}y_{43}^{\psi}\left\langle H_{t}\right\rangle}\approx\frac{s_{25}^{Q}\left|y_{53}^{\psi}\right|\left\langle H_{t}\right\rangle+m_{c}}{m_{t}}\approx s_{25}^{Q}\left|y_{53}^{\psi}\right|\approx\mathcal{O}(V_{cb})\,,

(553)

where we have considered
y_{43}^{\psi}=1
,
s_{34}^{Q}\approx 1
,
as required to explain the top mass (see the discussion in the first
bullet point in Section
20.3
)
and we have neglected the
m_{c}/m_{t}
small factor. This way, we have taken advantage
of the new contribution via the 5th family (and of the different hierarchies

m_{c}/m_{t}
and
m_{s}/m_{b}
) to cancel the dangerous down-quark mixing
while preserving the CKM via up-quark mixing.

•

The situation in the lepton sector is similar due to Pati-Salam universality
of the parameters, i.e.

\frac{-s_{25}^{L}\left|y_{53}^{\psi}\right|\left\langle H_{\tau}\right\rangle+c_{25}^{L}s_{34}^{e^{c}}y_{24}^{\psi}\left\langle H_{\mu}\right\rangle}{s_{34}^{L}y_{43}^{\psi}\left\langle H_{\tau}\right\rangle}\approx\frac{-s_{25}^{L}\left|y_{53}^{\psi}\right|\left\langle H_{\tau}\right\rangle+m_{\mu}}{m_{\tau}}\approx s_{25}^{L}\left|y_{53}^{\psi}\right|\approx\mathcal{O}(V_{cb})\,.

(554)

However, the leptonic mixing angles
s_{24}^{e^{c}}
and
s_{34}^{e^{c}}

are smaller than the quark ones if we assume the phenomenological
relation
\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\gg\left\langle\phi_{1}\right\rangle
.
This leads to
\left\langle H_{\mu}\right\rangle
being above the
scale of the muon mass, which predicts a quick growth of lepton mixing
in the scenario
s_{34}^{e^{c}}>s_{24}^{e^{c}}
. This can be easily
achieved in natural benchmarks. In this scenario, interesting signals
arise in LFV processes such as
\tau\rightarrow 3\mu
or
\tau\rightarrow\mu\gamma
,
mediated at tree-level by the
Z^{\prime}
boson, see Section
24.4
.

Other than the bullet points above, the mass matrices in Eqs. (
547
),
(
548
), (
549
)
lead to similar predictions as those of the simplified model in Section
20.3
.

23.3
Vector-fermion interactions in the extended model

In this section we shall compute the vector-fermion couplings involving
the heavy gauge bosons
U_{1}
,
g^{\prime}
,
Z^{\prime}
for the extended twin
Pati-Salam model. We omit the couplings of the vector-like partners in
the conjugate representations
\overline{\psi}_{\alpha}
and
\overline{\psi_{\alpha}^{c}}
,
since they do not mix with SM fermions.

U_{1}
couplings

In the original interaction basis, the vector leptoquark couples to the
heavy
SU(2)_{L}
doublets via the left-handed interactions
37
37

37

Notice that we continue working in the 2-component notation introduced
in Appendix
8
.
,

\mathcal{L}_{U_{1}}^{\mathrm{gauge}}=\frac{g_{4}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(Q_{4}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}L_{4}+Q_{5}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}L_{5}+Q_{6}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}L_{6}+\mathrm{h.c.}\right)U_{1}^{\mu}\,,

(555)

where similar couplings to the heavy
SU(2)_{L}
singlets
\psi^{c}

are also present, however they lead to suppressed couplings to SM
fermions due to the messenger dominance in Eq. (
546
).
This way, we obtain dominantly left-handed
U_{1}
couplings in good
approximation. Now we shall apply the unitary transformations in Eq. (
534
)
to rotate the fields from the original interaction basis to the decoupling
basis (primed),

\mathcal{L}_{U_{1}}^{\mathrm{gauge}}=\frac{g_{4}}{\sqrt{2}}Q^{\prime}{}_{\alpha}^{\dagger}V_{Q}\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}\mathrm{diag}(0,0,0,1,1,1)V_{L}^{\dagger}L^{\prime}_{\beta}U_{1}^{\mu}+\mathrm{h.c.}\,,

(556)

where

V_{Q}=V_{16}^{Q}V_{35}^{Q}V_{25}^{Q}V_{34}^{Q}V_{45}^{Q}\,,\qquad V_{L}=V_{16}^{L}V_{35}^{L}V_{25}^{L}V_{34}^{L}V_{45}^{L}\,.

(557)

The 4-5 rotations are different for quarks and leptons due to
\left\langle\Omega_{15}\right\rangle

splitting the mass terms of the vector-like fermions. They lead to a non-trivial
CKM-like matrix for the
U_{1}
couplings,

W_{LQ}=V_{45}^{Q}V_{45}^{L\dagger}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}c_{\theta_{LQ}}&-s_{\theta_{LQ}}&0\\
s_{\theta_{LQ}}&c_{\theta_{LQ}}&0\\
0&0&1\end{array}\right)\,,

(558)

where
s_{\theta_{LQ}}
depends on the angles
s_{45}^{Q}
and
s_{45}^{L}
,
obtained from the diagonalisation in Eq. (
23.2
).
The unitary matrix
W_{LQ}
can be regarded as a generalisation of
the CKM matrix to
SU(4)^{I}_{PS}
or quark-lepton flavour space. Similarly
to the CKM case, the
W_{LQ}
matrix is the only source of flavour-changing
transitions among
SU(4)^{I}_{PS}
states, and it appears only in interactions
mediated by
U_{1}
. In this sense, the vector leptoquark
U_{1}

is analogous to the SM
W^{\pm}
bosons. Similarly, the
Z^{\prime}
and
g^{\prime}

are analogous to the SM
Z
boson, and we will show that their interactions
are
SU(4)^{I}_{PS}
flavour-conserving at tree-level. In analogy to the
SM, we will denote
U_{1}
transitions as charged currents and
Z^{\prime}
,

g^{\prime}
transitions as neutral currents. As in the SM, FCNCs proportional
to the
W_{LQ}
matrix are generated at loop level. This mechanism
was firstly identified in
[
268
]
for a similar 4321
framework.

The same mixing that leads to the SM fermion masses and mixings, see
Eq. (
557
), also leads to effective
U_{1}

couplings to SM fermions which can contribute to the LFU ratios,

\mathcal{L}_{U_{1}}^{\mathrm{gauge}}=\frac{g_{4}}{\sqrt{2}}Q^{\prime}{}_{i}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}s_{16}^{Q}s_{16}^{L}\epsilon&0&0\\
0&c_{\theta_{LQ}}s_{25}^{Q}s_{25}^{L}&s_{\theta_{LQ}}s_{25}^{Q}s_{34}^{L}\\
0&-s_{\theta_{LQ}}s_{34}^{Q}s_{25}^{L}&c_{\theta_{LQ}}s_{34}^{Q}s_{34}^{L}\end{array}\right)L^{\prime}_{j}U_{1}^{\mu}+\mathrm{h.c.}\,,

(559)

where we have considered that
s_{35}^{Q,L}
are small, see Section
s

23.2

and
24.3
. The first family coupling can
be diluted via mixing with vector-like fermions, which is parameterised
via the effective parameter
\epsilon
(see Section
24.4

for more details
)
. The couplings above receive small corrections
due to 2-3 fermion mixing arising after diagonalising the effective
mass matrices in Eqs. (
547
), (
548
), (
549
).
It can be seen from Eq. (
559
) that a large (2,3)
coupling
\beta_{c\nu_{\tau}}
arises now, proportional to the large
sines
s_{\theta_{LQ}}
,
s_{34}^{L}
and
s_{25}^{Q}
. This solves
one important issue of the simplified model, where the flavour-violating
couplings
\beta_{c\nu_{\tau}}
and
\beta_{b\mu}
where connected
to small 2-3 mixing angles, suppressing the contributions of
U_{1}

to the LFU ratios. In any case, the leptoquark couplings that contribute
to
B
-decays arise due to the same mixing effects which diagonalise
the mass matrices of the model, yielding mass terms for the SM fermions.
This way, the flavour puzzle and the
B
-anomalies are dynamically
and parametrically connected in this model, leading to a predictive
framework. Notice that thanks to the connection with the flavour puzzle, our

U_{1}
couplings are dominantly left-handed, in contrast with the alternative
theories in the market
[
115
,
116
,
117
,
119
,
120
]
.

Coloron couplings and GIM-like mechanism

In the original interaction basis, the coloron couplings are flavour-diagonal,
featuring the following couplings to
SU(2)_{L}
doublets,

\mathcal{L}_{g^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{gauge}}=\frac{g_{4}g_{s}}{g_{3}}\left(Q_{4}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}T^{a}Q_{4}+Q_{5}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}T^{a}Q_{5}+Q_{6}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}T^{a}Q_{6}-\frac{g_{3}^{2}}{g_{4}^{2}}Q_{i}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}T^{a}Q_{i}\right)g^{\prime}{}_{\mu}^{a}\,,

(560)

where
i=1,2,3
. Now we rotate to the decoupling basis by applying
the transformations in Eq. (
557
), (assuming
small
x_{35}^{\psi}
as discussed in Section
24.3
)
obtaining

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{gauge}}

\displaystyle=\frac{g_{4}g_{s}}{g_{3}}Q^{\prime}{}_{i}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}T^{a}

(561)

\displaystyle\times\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\left(s_{16}^{Q}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{16}^{Q}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{3}^{2}}{g_{4}^{2}}&0&0\\
0&\left(s_{25}^{Q}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{25}^{Q}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{3}^{2}}{g_{4}^{2}}&0\\
0&0&\left(s_{34}^{Q}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{34}^{Q}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{3}^{2}}{g_{4}^{2}}\end{array}\right)Q^{\prime}_{j}g^{\prime}{}_{\mu}^{a}\,,

Here
V_{45}^{Q}
cancels due to unitarity and due to the coloron couplings
between vector-like quarks being flavour universal in the original basis of
Eq. (
560
). Therefore, as anticipated, the
CKM-like matrix
W_{LQ}
does not affect the neutral currents mediated
by
g^{\prime}
(and similarly for the
Z^{\prime}
boson). The coloron couplings in Eq. (
561
)
receive small corrections due to 2-3 mixing arising after diagonalising
the effective mass matrices in Eqs. (
547
), (
548
), (
549
),
predominantly in the up sector due to the down-aligned flavour structure
achieved in Section
23.2
. We obtain
similar couplings for
SU(2)_{L}
singlets, however their mixing angles
are suppressed by the messenger dominance in Eq. (
546
),
and so they remain as in the original interaction basis to good approximation.

The coloron couplings of Eq. (
561
) are
family universal if

s_{34}^{Q}=s_{25}^{Q}=s_{16}^{Q}\,,

(565)

leading to a GIM-like protection from tree-level FCNCs mediated by
the coloron. The condition above was already identified in
[
268
]
,
denoted as
full alignment limit
. However, we have seen that
maximal
s_{34}^{Q}\approx 1
is well motivated in our model to protect
the perturbativity of the top Yukawa, by the fit of the
R_{D^{(*)}}

anomaly, and furthermore it naturally suppresses
s_{35}^{Q}
via
a small
c_{34}^{Q}
. The caveat is that if the condition in Eq. (
565
)
is implemented, then
s_{16}^{Q}
and
s_{25}^{Q}
would also be
maximal, leading to large couplings to valence quarks which would
blow up the production of the coloron at the LHC. This fact was already
identified in
[
268
]
, where large
s_{34}^{Q}
was
also suggested by the
B
-anomalies, and this motivated a partial
alignment limit,

s_{25}^{Q}=s_{16}^{Q}\,,

(566)

which suppresses FCNCs between the first and second quark families,
proportional to the largest off-diagonal elements of the CKM matrix. FCNCs between
the second and third families still arise, however we are protected
from the stringent constraints of
B_{s}-\bar{B}_{s}
meson mixing
due to the down-aligned flavour structure achieved in Section
23.2
.
Finally, FCNCs between the first and third families are also under control,
as they are proportional to the smaller elements of the CKM matrix.

The GIM-like condition of Eq. (
566
) translates,
in terms of fundamental parameters of our model, into

\frac{x_{25}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{25}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{5}^{Q}\right)^{2}}}=\frac{x_{16}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{\left(x_{16}^{\psi}\left\langle\phi_{3}\right\rangle\right)^{2}+\left(M_{6}^{Q}\right)^{2}}}\,,

(567)

which could be naively achieved with natural couplings and
M_{5}^{Q}
,

M_{6}^{Q}
being of the same order, as allowed by the messenger
dominance in Eq. (
546
).
The couplings and vector-like mass terms can also be chosen differently, as far
as Eq. (
567
) is preserved. At the moment, the GIM-like
mechanism is accidental. However, Eq. (
567
) suggests
that the sixth and fifth family, and also the first and second families,
might transform as doublets under a global
SU(2)
symmetry, enforcing
the parametric relations of Eq. (
567
).

Z^{\prime}
couplings

We can follow the same procedure to extract the couplings of the
Z^{\prime}

boson to chiral fermions,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\prime},q}^{\mathrm{gauge}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{g_{4}g_{Y}}{g_{1}}Q^{\prime}{}_{i}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}

(568)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{1}{6}\left(s_{16}^{Q}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{16}^{Q}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{9g_{4}^{2}}&0&0\\
0&\frac{1}{6}\left(s_{25}^{Q}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{25}^{Q}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{9g_{4}^{2}}&0\\
0&0&\frac{1}{6}\left(s_{34}^{Q}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{34}^{Q}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{9g_{4}^{2}}\end{array}\right)Q^{\prime}_{j}Z^{\prime}_{\mu}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\prime},\ell}^{\mathrm{gauge}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{g_{4}g_{Y}}{g_{1}}L^{\prime}{}_{i}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}

(572)

\displaystyle\times\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{1}{2}\left(s_{16}^{L}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{16}^{L}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{3g_{4}^{2}}&0&0\\
0&\frac{1}{2}\left(s_{25}^{L}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{25}^{L}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{3g_{4}^{2}}&0\\
0&0&\frac{1}{2}\left(s_{34}^{L}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{34}^{L}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{3g_{4}^{2}}\end{array}\right)L^{\prime}_{j}Z^{\prime}_{\mu}\,,

where the up-quark couplings above receive small corrections due
to 2-3 mixing arising after diagonalising the effective mass matrices
in Eqs. (
547
), (
548
).
However, larger 2-3 charged lepton mixing is possible (see Section
23.2
),
obtaining for charged leptons:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{Z^{\prime},e}^{\mathrm{gauge}}\approx-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{g_{4}g_{Y}}{g_{1}}\hat{e}{}_{i}^{\dagger}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}

(576)

\displaystyle\times\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{1}{2}\left(s_{16}^{L}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{16}^{L}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{3g_{4}^{2}}&0&0\\
0&\frac{1}{2}\left(s_{25}^{L}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{25}^{L}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{3g_{4}^{2}}&\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(s_{34}^{L}\right)^{2}-\left(s_{25}^{L}\right)^{2}\right]s_{23}^{e}\\
0&\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(s_{34}^{L}\right)^{2}-\left(s_{25}^{L}\right)^{2}\right]s_{23}^{e}&\frac{1}{2}\left(s_{34}^{L}\right)^{2}-\left(c_{34}^{L}\right)^{2}\frac{g_{1}^{2}}{3g_{4}^{2}}\end{array}\right)\hat{e}_{j}Z^{\prime}_{\mu}\,,

at first order in
s^{e}_{23}
and taking
c^{e}_{23}\approx 1
. The flavour-violating couplings above can lead to interesting signals in LFV processes such as
\tau\rightarrow 3\mu

and
\tau\rightarrow\mu\gamma
, see more in Section
24.4
.

In order to suppress LFV between the first and second lepton families,
a similar condition similar to Eq. (
566
)
but for leptons can be implemented,

s_{25}^{L}=s_{16}^{L}\,.

(580)

Remarkably, if the condition of Eq. (
566
)
is fulfilled, then Eq. (
580
) would also
be fulfilled in good approximation thanks to the underlying twin Pati-Salam
symmetry, the small breaking effects given by the splitting of vector-like
masses via
\left\langle\Omega_{15}\right\rangle
.

24
Phenomenology of the extended model

The twin Pati-Salam model features a fermiophobic low-energy 4321 theory with
a rich phenomenology. Although extensive analyses of general 4321
models have been performed during the last few years, the vast majority
of them have been performed in the framework of non-fermiophobic 4321
models
[
262
,
263
,
356
,
115
,
170
]
.
Instead, the twin Pati-Salam model offers a fermiophobic scenario with a different
phenomenology. Being a theory of flavour, extra constraints and correlations
arise via the generation of the SM Yukawa couplings and the prediction
of fermion masses and mixing, including striking signals in LFV processes. Moreover,
the underlying twin Pati-Salam symmetry introduces universality (and
perturbativity) constraints over several parameters, which are not
present in alternative models. These features motivate a dedicated analysis.
We will highlight key observables for which the intrinsic nature of
the model can be disentangled from all alternative proposals. All low-energy observables considered are listed in Table
14
,
with references to current experimental bounds and links to theory
expressions.

:
Input and output parameters for the benchmark points BP1 and BP2,
* indicates BP1 while ** indicates BP2, otherwise both benchmarks
share the same parameters. BP1 is compatible with 2021 data on
R_{K^{(*)}}
,
while BP2 is compatible with the 2022 updates by LHCb.

In the forthcoming sections we will assume the couplings of the fundamental
Lagrangian to be universal, such as
x_{34}^{\psi}
and
x_{25}^{\psi}
,
however their universality is broken by small RGE effects which we
estimate in Section
24.7
to be below 8%.
We neglect the small RGE effects and preserve universal parameters
for the phenomenological analysis, in order to simplify the exploration
of the parameter space and highlight the underlying twin Pati-Salam
symmetry.

24.1
R_{D^{(*)}}
and
R_{K^{(*)}}

In our model, the left-handed WCs
\left[C_{\nu edu}^{*}\right]^{\tau\tau 32}

and
C_{9}^{\mu\mu}=-C_{10}^{\mu\mu}
are obtained at tree-level
after integrating out the heavy gauge bosons, with the overall contribution
being dominated by
U_{1}
tree-level exchange as in Fig.
30
,

\left[C_{\nu edu}^{*}\right]^{\tau\tau 32}(m_{b})=\frac{2\eta_{V}^{\nu\tau}}{V_{cb}}\left[C_{lq}^{(3)}\right]^{\tau\tau 23}(\Lambda)\,,

(581)

C_{9}^{\mu\mu}(m_{b})=-C_{10}^{\mu\mu}(m_{b})=-\frac{2\pi}{\alpha_{\mathrm{EM}}V_{tb}V_{ts}^{*}}\eta_{V}^{\ell\ell}\left(\left[C_{lq}^{(3)}\right]^{\mu\mu 23}(\Lambda)+\left[C_{lq}^{(1)}\right]^{\mu\mu 23}(\Lambda)\right)\,,

(582)

where the negligible RGE effect is encoded as
\eta_{V}^{\nu\tau}\approx 1.00144
,

\eta_{V}^{\ell\ell}\approx 0.974
and has been computed with
DsixTools 2.1

[
174
]
for
\Lambda=1\,\mathrm{TeV}
.
Notice also the tree-level matching of the twin Pati-Salam model to the SMEFT
in Section
21
. In order to be compatible
with the SM-like
R_{K^{(*)}}
ratios, constraints over
C_{9}^{\mu\mu}=-C_{10}^{\mu\mu}

are shown in Table
14
. Similarly, in order to
explain the
R_{D^{(*)}}
anomalies there is a preferred region for
the
\left[C_{\nu edu}^{*}\right]^{\tau\tau 32}
coefficient, as shown in Table
14
.
The full EFT description of these observables (including the definition
of the Wilson coefficients) plus a discussion of current experimental
data can be found in Sections
13.1
and
13.2
.

In terms of fundamental parameters of the model, the deviations from
the SM in the LFU ratios scale as follows,

\left|\Delta R_{D^{(*)}}\right|\propto\left|\left(x_{34}^{\psi}\right)^{3}x_{25}^{\psi}\right|\,,

(583)

\left|\Delta R_{K^{(*)}}\right|\propto\left|x_{34}^{\psi}\left(x_{25}^{\psi}\right)^{3}\right|\,,

(584)

where we have fixed the vector-like masses and the 4321-breaking VEVs to the
values of our benchmark in Table
15
. This way, the Yukawa
couplings above control the contributions to most of the relevant
phenomenology, including the LFU ratios. The Pati-Salam universality
of
x_{34}^{\psi}
and
x_{25}^{\psi}
provides here a welcome constraint,
not present in other 4321 models. In particular, one can see that
both
R_{D^{(*)}}
and
R_{K^{(*)}}
are connected via the same
parameters and deviations in both are expected, while in general 4321 models
(such as the fermiophobic model of Refs.
[
267
,
268
]
) the analog of a single
x_{i\alpha}^{\psi}
decomposes into several
parameters for quarks and leptons, which decouple
R_{K^{(*)}}
from

R_{D^{(*)}}
.

Following from Eqs. (
583
) and (
584
),
the cubic dependence of
R_{K^{(*)}}
on
x_{25}^{\psi}
anticipates
that we can suppress the contribution to
R_{K^{(*)}}
, while preserving
a large contribution to
R_{D^{(*)}}
thanks to its linear dependence
on
x_{25}^{\psi}
. As a consequence, the yellow band of parameter
space preferred by 2022
R_{K^{(*)}}
is just shifted below the orange
band of 2021
R_{K^{(*)}}
in Fig.
.
The 2022
R_{K^{(*)}}
band is compatible with
R_{D^{(*)}}
at

1\sigma
only in a narrow region of the parameter space. This is
encouraging, given the fact that the model was built to address the
2021 tensions in both LFU ratios. However, in order to explain
R_{D^{(*)}}
,
small deviations from the SM in the
R_{K^{(*)}}
ratios are unavoidable,
to be tested in the future via more precise measurements of LFU by
the LHCb collaboration. Moreover, lower central values for
R_{D^{(*)}}

are also expected.

Remarkably, the fact that the twin Pati-Salam model only generates the effective
operator
(\bar{c}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}b_{L})

(\bar{\text{$\tau$}}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\nu_{\tau L})

implies that both
R_{D}
and
R_{D^{*}}
are corrected in the same
direction and with the same size, i.e.
\Delta R_{D}=\Delta R_{D^{*}}
.
Instead, non-fermiophobic 4321 models also predict the scalar operator

(\bar{c}_{L}b_{R})(\bar{\text{$\tau$}}_{R}\nu_{\tau L})
,
which leads to a larger correction for
R_{D}
than that of
R_{D^{*}}

(about 5/2 larger for the
\mathrm{PS}^{3}
model, see Eq. (27)
in
[
115
]
). Current data is equally compatible with both
according to recent global fits
[
269
]
.

24.2
Off-shell photon penguin with tau leptons

As discussed in Section
13.12
, the SMEFT
scenario
\left[C_{lq}^{(1)}\right]^{\tau\tau 23}=\left[C_{lq}^{(3)}\right]^{\tau\tau 23}

correlates
R_{D^{(*)}}
with a large contribution to
C_{9}^{\tau\tau}=-C_{10}^{\tau\tau}
,
which then mixes into a lepton universal contribution to the operator
C_{9}

via RGE effects. The
U_{1}
model is an specific example of this
scenario, where the same leptoquark couplings that explain
R_{D^{(*)}}

are correlated to those in
b\rightarrow s\tau\tau
due to
SU(2)_{L}

invariance. The specific contributions to
b\rightarrow s\tau\tau

will be explored in Section
24.6
,
but in this section we explore the impact of the universal contribution
to
\mathcal{O}_{9}^{\ell\ell}
, denoted as
C_{9}^{U}
, over the
anomalous
b\rightarrow s\mu\mu
transition. The contribution of
U_{1}
to

C_{9}^{U}
originates from a 1-loop off-shell photon penguin diagram
with tau leptons running in the loop, shown in Fig.
.
Notice that this diagram is similar to that of Fig.
but exchanging
the insertion of the 4-fermion operator by the insertion of the
U_{1}

leptoquark in the loop, plus adding new contributions provided by
the vector-like leptons of our model running in the loop. We finally obtain

b_{L}
\ell^{+}
\ell^{-}
s_{L}
U_{1}
\tau_{L},\,E_{L4},\,E_{L5}
\gamma
\tau_{L},\,E_{L4},\,E_{L5}

(a)

(b)

Figure 32
:
Left:

Off-shell photon penguin with tau
leptons in the loop that generates
C_{9}^{U}
, a contribution to
the lepton universal operator
\mathcal{O}_{9}^{23\ell\ell}
that
participates in
b\rightarrow s\ell\ell
transitions.
Right:

C_{9}^{U}
as a function of
x_{25}^{\psi}
via Eq. (
585
),
with
x_{34}^{\psi}
varied in the range [1, 3.5] as preferred
by
R_{D^{(*)}}
(blue region), with the rest of parameters fixed
as in Table
15
. The grey (light grey) region denotes the
1
\sigma
(
2\sigma
) contour of
C_{9}^{U}
as preferred by a
global fit to
b\rightarrow s\ell\ell
data taken from
[
359
]
.
The yellow (orange) band denotes the
1\sigma
region preferred by

R_{K^{(*)}}^{2022}
(
R_{K^{(*)}}^{2021}
). The blue and red stars
denote BP1 and BP2 respectively.

\displaystyle C_{9}^{U}=-\frac{v_{\mathrm{SM}}^{2}g_{4}^{2}}{6V_{tb}V_{ts}^{*}M_{U_{\text{1}}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\left(\log\left[\frac{2m_{b}^{2}}{g_{4}^{2}M_{U_{1}}^{2}}\right]\beta_{s\tau}\beta_{b\tau}^{*}+\log\left[\frac{2m_{E_{5}}^{2}}{g_{4}^{2}M_{U_{1}}^{2}}\right]\beta_{sE_{5}}\beta_{bE_{5}}^{*}\right.

(585)

\displaystyle\left.+\log\left[\frac{2m_{E_{4}}^{2}}{g_{4}^{2}M_{U_{1}}^{2}}\right]\beta_{sE_{4}}\beta_{bE_{4}}^{*}\right)\,,

which is explicitly correlated to
b\rightarrow s\tau\tau
, as well
as to
R_{D^{(*)}}
since
SU(2)_{L}
invariance implies
\beta_{s\tau}\approx\beta_{c\nu_{\tau}}

for the
U_{1}
couplings. Therefore, the scaling is
|C_{9}^{U}|\propto(x_{34}^{\psi})^{3}x_{25}^{\psi}
,
just like
R_{D^{(*)}}
. Notice that the contribution of our model
to
C_{9}^{U}
differs from those of alternative models in the literature

[
291
]

due to the vector-like leptons
E_{4,5}
running in the loop. Unfortunately,
due to the flavour structure of our model, the contributions via vector-like
leptons interfere negatively with the leading contribution via the
tau loop, and hence our overall contribution to
C_{9}^{U}
is generally
smaller than in the alternative models. The contribution from
E_{4}

is negligible, but the contribution from
E_{5}
reduces
C_{9}^{U}

by a 20% factor of the tau loop contribution.

In our model,
R_{D^{(*)}}
and
R_{K^{(*)}}
are correlated, as
can be seen from Eqs. (
583
) and (
584
).
Therefore,
C_{9}^{U}
is not only correlated with
R_{D^{(*)}}

but also with
R_{K^{(*)}}
. Given that deviations from 1 in
R_{K^{(*)}}

are now constrained by the new LHCb measurements, our final contribution
to
C_{9}^{U}
is constrained to be
C_{9}^{U}\approx-0.4
, as can
be seen in Fig.
. However, global fits of
b\rightarrow s\ell\ell

data (see e.g.
[
359
,
188
,
82
]
),
mostly driven by anomalies in
\mathrm{Br}(B\rightarrow K\mu\mu)
,

\mathrm{Br}(B_{s}\rightarrow\phi\mu\mu)
and
P^{\prime}_{5}(B\rightarrow K^{*}\mu\mu)

(see the discussion in Section
13.1
), prefer a larger
value
C_{9}^{U}\approx-0.8
. Therefore, we conclude that our model
is not able to fully address the anomalies in
b\rightarrow s\ell\ell

via the off-shell photon penguin, although our contribution
to
C_{9}^{U}
ameliorates the tensions. Performing a more
ambitious analysis would require to make assumptions about the hadronic
uncertainties afflicting
\mathrm{Br}(B\rightarrow K\mu\mu)
,
\mathrm{Br}(B_{s}\rightarrow\phi\mu\mu)

and
P^{\prime}_{5}(B\rightarrow K^{*}\mu\mu)
, which is beyond the scope
of this work.

However, we notice that in principle our model has the ideal structure
to the address all the anomalies in
B
-physics, i.e. we reproduce
the preferred scenario presented in
[
82
]
(see also
Section
13.12
) where a large
contribution to
\left[C_{\nu edu}^{*}\right]^{\tau\tau 32}
addresses
R_{D^{(*)}}

and ameliorates tensions in
b\rightarrow s\mu\mu
data, while a
small contribution to
C_{9}^{\mu\mu}=-C_{10}^{\mu\mu}
further
improves the overall fit. Even though our contribution to
C_{9}^{U}

does not reach the preferred values by the global fits, it improves
the overall description of
b\rightarrow s\mu\mu
data with respect
to the SM.

24.3
B_{s}-\bar{B}_{s}
mixing

In the extended twin Pati-Salam model, tree-level contributions to
B_{s}-\bar{B}_{s}

mixing via 2-3 quark mixing are suppressed due to the down-aligned
flavour structure achieved in Section
23.2
.
A further 1-loop contribution mediated by
U_{1}
has been studied
in the literature
[
268
,
263
,
360
]

for other 4321 models, and vector-like charged leptons are known to
play a crucial role. In Ref.
[
268
]
, a framework
with three vector-like charged leptons was considered, however the loop function
was generalised from the SM
W^{\pm}
box diagram, so the bounds were expected
to be slightly overestimated. Instead, in
[
360
]

the proper loop function was derived, but a framework with only one
vector-like charged lepton was considered. For this work, we have generalised
the loop function of
[
360
]
to the case of
three vector-like leptons. The 1-loop contribution to the effective Wilson
coefficient
C_{1}^{bs}
(see Section
13.4
) mediated
by
U_{1}
reads,

\left.C_{1}^{bs}\right|_{\mathrm{NP}}^{\mathrm{loop}}=\frac{g_{4}^{4}}{\left(8\pi M_{U_{1}}\right)^{2}}\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\left(\beta_{s\alpha}^{*}\beta_{b\alpha}\right)\left(\beta_{s\beta}^{*}\beta_{b\beta}\right)F(x_{\alpha},x_{\beta})\,,

(586)

where
\alpha,\beta=\mu,\tau,E_{4},E_{5}
run for all charged leptons,
including the vector-like partners (except for electrons and the sixth
charged lepton which do not couple to the second or third generation),
and
x_{\alpha}=(m_{\alpha}/M_{U_{1}})^{2}
. The contribution corresponds
to the box diagrams in Fig.
33
. The product
of couplings
\beta_{s\alpha}^{*}\beta_{b\alpha}
has the fundamental
property

\sum_{\alpha}\beta_{s\alpha}^{*}\beta_{b\alpha}=0\,,

(587)

which arises from unitarity of the transformations in Eq. (
557
).
This property, similarly to the GIM mechanism in the SM, is essential
to render the loop finite. However, the property holds as long as
the 2-3 down mixing and
s_{35}^{Q}
are small. In particular,
s_{35}^{Q}

is naturally small in the scenario
s_{34}^{Q}\approx 1
, as it is
suppressed by the small cosine
c_{34}^{Q}
, see the definition of

s_{35}^{Q}
in Eq. (
538
). Ultimately, the mixing
angle
s_{35}^{Q}
is controlled by the fundamental parameter
x_{35}^{\psi}
,
and we obtained that
x_{35}^{\psi}\apprle 0.09
is required to pass
the
\Delta M_{s}
bound.

The loop function reads

s_{L}
\beta_{s\beta}^{*}
\beta_{b\beta}
b_{L}
\beta_{b\alpha}
\beta_{s\alpha}^{*}
b_{L}
s_{L}
U_{1}
\ell_{L\beta}
U_{1}
\ell_{L\alpha}

(a)

s_{L}
\beta_{s\alpha}^{*}
\beta_{b\beta}
b_{L}
\beta_{b\alpha}
\beta_{s\beta}^{*}
b_{L}
s_{L}
\ell_{L\alpha}
U_{1}
\ell_{L\beta}
U_{1}

(b)

Figure 33
:
U_{1}
-mediated 1-loop diagrams contributing to
B_{s}-\bar{B}_{s}

mixing. The indices
\alpha,\beta
run for all charged leptons including
vector-like, i.e.
\ell_{L\alpha}=\left(\mu_{L},\tau_{L},E_{L4},E_{L5}\right)
.

F(x_{\alpha},x_{\beta})=\left(1+\frac{x_{\alpha}x_{\beta}}{4}\right)B(x_{\alpha},x_{\beta})\,,

(588)

where

B(x_{\alpha},x_{\beta})=\frac{1}{\left(1-x_{\alpha}\right)\left(1-x_{\beta}\right)}+\frac{x_{\alpha}^{2}\log x_{\alpha}}{\left(x_{\beta}-x_{\alpha}\right)\left(1-x_{\alpha}^{2}\right)}+\frac{x_{\beta}^{2}\log x_{\beta}}{\left(x_{\alpha}-x_{\beta}\right)\left(1-x_{\beta}^{2}\right)}\,.

(589)

In this manner, the loop function is dominated by the vector-like
partners because they are much heavier than chiral charged leptons.
In particular, in the motivated scenario with maximal
s_{34}^{L}
,
the couplings with the fourth family
\beta_{sE_{4}}^{*}\beta_{bE_{4}}

are suppressed by the small cosine
c_{34}^{L}
. This way, the loop
is dominated by
E_{5}
in good approximation. We obtain the effective
loop function in this scenario by removing all constants in
x_{\alpha,\beta}
,
which vanish due to the property (
587
),

\tilde{F}(x)\approx F(x,x)-2F(x,0)+F(0,0)=\frac{x\left(x+4\right)\left(-1+x^{2}-2x\log x\right)}{4\left(x-1\right)^{3}}\,,

(590)

and in this approximation the contribution to
C_{1}^{bs}
reads

(a)

(b)

Figure 34
:
Left:

\delta(\Delta M_{s})
(Eq. (

))
as a function of the 5th vector-like mass term.

is

(

)
preferred by
R_{K^{(*)}}^{2022}
(

), obtaining
the yellow (blue) band. The grey region is excluded by the
\Delta M_{s}

bound, see Eq. (

).
Right:

Parameter space in the plane (

,

)
compatible with
R_{D^{(*)}}
and

at 1

. The
remaining parameters are fixed as in Table
15
for both

. The

bound, the region excluded
only due to the contribution via the 5th lepton is also shown in lighter
blue for comparison. The blue and red stars denote BP1 and BP2 respectively.

\left.C_{1}^{bs}\right|_{\mathrm{NP}}^{\mathrm{loop}}=\frac{g_{4}^{4}}{\left(8\pi M_{U_{1}}\right)^{2}}\left(\beta_{sE_{5}}^{*}\beta_{bE_{5}}\right)^{2}\tilde{F}(x_{E_{5}})\,.

(591)

The loop function grows with
x_{E_{5}}
. However, in the limit of
large bare mass term
M_{5}^{L}
the effective coupling
\beta_{sE_{5}}^{*}\propto s_{25}^{Q}

vanishes (since large
M_{5}^{L}
also implies large
M_{5}^{Q}

due to the Pati-Salam symmetry), hence both the contribution to
C_{1}^{bs}

and
R_{D^{(*)}}
go away. In Fig.
we plot

\delta(\Delta M_{s})
defined in Eq. (
216
)
in terms of
M_{5}^{L}
, and we vary
x_{25}^{\psi}
in the ranges
compatible with
R_{D^{(*)}}
and
R_{K^{(*)}}^{2022}
(
R_{K^{(*)}}^{2021}
).
We can see that the
\Delta M_{s}
bound requires a vector-like lepton
around 1.5-2 TeV in the 2022 case, while 2021 data was pointing to
a vector-like lepton with a mass around 1 TeV.

In Fig.
we show that Eq. (
591
)
is indeed a good approximation, up to small interference effects between
the 4th and 5th family contributions in the small
x_{34}^{\psi}

region, where the fourth lepton is lighter. We also show the parameter
space compatible with
\Delta M_{s}
and the LFU ratios in our benchmark
scenario. In particular,
\Delta M_{s}
turns out to be the strongest
constraint over the parameter space other than
R_{K^{(*)}}^{2022}
.

24.4
LFV processes

\boldsymbol{\tau\rightarrow 3\mu}

The partial alignment condition of Eq. (
580
)
allows for
Z^{\prime}
-mediated FCNCs in
\tau\mu
processes, due to the
fact that the model predicts significant mixing between the muon and
tau charged leptons. This is a crucial prediction of the twin Pati-Salam theory
of flavour, not present in general 4321 models. Of particular interest
is the process
\tau\rightarrow 3\mu
, which receives a tree-level

Z^{\prime}
-mediated contribution that grows with the
\tau\mu
mixing angle
s_{23}^{e}
.
Beyond the latter,
\tau\rightarrow 3\mu
also receives a
U_{1}
-mediated
1-loop contribution

\left.\left[C_{ee}^{V,LL}\right]^{\mu\tau\mu\mu}\right|_{U_{1}}^{\mathrm{loop}}=\frac{3g_{4}^{4}}{128\pi^{2}M_{U_{1}}^{2}}\beta_{D_{5}\mu}^{*}\beta_{D_{5}\tau}\left(\beta_{D_{5}\mu}\right)^{2}\tilde{F}(x_{D_{5}})\,.

(592)

The effective coupling
\beta_{D_{5}\mu}
is proportional to
s_{25}^{L}\approx 0.1
,
which provides a further suppression of
\mathcal{O}((s_{25}^{L})^{3})

that renders the loop negligible against the much larger tree-level

Z^{\prime}
-mediated contribution
\left.\left[C_{ee}^{V,LL}\right]^{\mu\tau\mu\mu}\right|_{Z^{\prime}}
,
that is obtained from the tree-level matching to the SMEFT in Section
21
.
The typical benchmark
s_{25}^{L}\approx 0.1
naturally suppresses
the
\mu\mu Z^{\prime}
coupling, keeping the
Z^{\prime}
contribution to
\tau\rightarrow 3\mu

under control, and simultaneously protects from
Z^{\prime}\rightarrow\mu\mu

dilepton searches at the LHC (see Section
24.8
).

(a)

(b)

Figure 35
:
Left:

\mathcal{B}\left(\tau\rightarrow 3\mu\right)

as a function of the 2-3 charged lepton mixing sine
s_{23}^{e}
.
The purple region denotes the
Z^{\prime}
contribution while the blue region
denotes the
U_{1}
contribution, for both we have varied
x_{25}^{\psi}=[0.3,0.35]

which is compatible with
R_{K^{(*)}}^{2022}
.
Right:

\mathcal{B}\left(\tau\rightarrow\mu\gamma\right)
as a function
of
x_{34}^{\psi}
. The purple region denotes the
Z^{\prime}
contribution
for which we have varied
s_{23}^{e}=[V_{cb},5V_{cb}]
. The blue
region denotes the
U_{1}
contribution, for which we have varied

x_{25}^{\psi}=[0.1,1]
. The grey regions are excluded by the experiment,
the dashed lines show the projected future bound. The red star shows
BP2.

As depicted in Fig.
, the
Z^{\prime}
contribution dominates
over the
U_{1}
contribution, and the regions of the parameter space
with very large
s_{23}^{e}
are already excluded by the experiment.
We have chosen to plot the results of the 2022 case only, since this
observable depends mostly on
s_{23}^{e}
and there is little variation
with 2021 data. The Belle II collaboration will test a further region
of the parameter space
[
255
]
, setting the bound

s_{23}^{e}<2.8V_{cb}
if no signal is detected. In general 4321
models (such as
[
267
,
268
,
262
,
263
]
)
the
\mu-\tau
mixing is unspecified, so only the small
U_{1}

signal is predicted. Therefore, the large
Z^{\prime}
signal offers the
opportunity to disentangle the twin Pati-Salam model from alternative
4321 proposals.

As depicted in Fig.
,
\tau\rightarrow 3\mu

is the most constraining signal over the parameter space out of all
the LFV processes, provided that the 2-3 charged lepton mixing is

\mathcal{O}(0.1)
.

\boldsymbol{\tau\rightarrow\mu\gamma}

The dipole operator
[\mathcal{O}_{e\gamma}]^{\mu\tau}
receives
1-loop contributions in our model via both
U_{1}
and
Z^{\prime}
,

[C_{e\gamma}]^{\mu\tau}=\left.[C_{e\gamma}]^{\mu\tau}\right|_{U_{1}}+\left.[C_{e\gamma}]^{\mu\tau}\right|_{Z^{\prime}}\,,

(593)

where

\left.[C_{e\gamma}]^{\mu\tau}\right|_{U_{1}}(\Lambda)=-\frac{C_{U}}{32\pi^{2}}\sum_{i}\beta_{i\mu}^{*}\beta_{i\tau}\left[G_{1}(x_{i})-2G_{2}(x_{i})\right]\,,

(594)

\left.[C_{e\gamma}]^{\mu\tau}\right|_{Z^{\prime}}(\Lambda)=-\frac{C_{Z^{\prime}}}{32\pi^{2}}\sum_{\alpha}\xi_{\tau\alpha}\xi_{\alpha\mu}\widetilde{G}(x_{\alpha})\,,

(595)

where
i=s,b,D_{4},D_{5}
and
\alpha=\mu,\tau,E_{4},E_{5}
. The
loop functions are given by
[
263
,
360
,
234
]

G_{1}(x)=x\left[\frac{2-5x}{2\left(x-1\right)^{4}}\log x-\frac{4-13x+3x^{2}}{4\left(x-1\right)^{3}}\right]\,,

(596)

G_{2}(x)=x\left[\frac{4x-1}{2\left(x-1\right)^{4}}x\log x-\frac{2-5x-3x^{2}}{4\left(x-1\right)^{3}}\right]\,,

(597)

\widetilde{G}(x)=\frac{5x^{4}-14x^{3}+39x^{2}-38x-18x^{2}\log x+8}{12(1-x)^{4}}\,.

(598)

The running of the dipole operator from
\Lambda=2\;\mathrm{TeV}

to the scale
\mu\sim m_{\tau}
is given by
[C_{e\gamma}]^{\mu\tau}(m_{\tau})\approx 0.92[C_{e\gamma}]^{\mu\tau}(\Lambda)
,
as estimated with
DsixTools 2.1

[
174
]
. Neglecting
the muon mass, the branching ratio
\mathcal{B}(\tau\rightarrow\mu\gamma)

is given by Eq. (
240
).

Provided that the 3-4 mixing is maximal, the
U_{1}
loop is dominated
by the 5th vector-like quark, and in this situation the couplings

\beta_{D_{5}\mu}^{*}\beta_{D_{5}\tau}
are controlled by
x_{25}^{\psi}
.
The
Z^{\prime}
loop is dominated by chiral leptons, in particular by the

\tau
lepton, since the coupling
\xi_{\tau\tau}
is maximal while

\xi_{\mu\mu}
is suppressed. In this scenario, the overall
Z^{\prime}

contribution is controlled by
\xi_{\tau\mu}
which grows with the

\mu-\tau
mixing angle
s_{23}^{e}
, and the variation via
x_{25}^{\psi}

is minimal.

\tau_{R}
\beta_{i\tau}
\beta_{i\mu}^{*}
\mu_{L}
\tau_{L}
U_{1}
d_{Li}

(a)

\tau_{R}
\xi_{\tau\alpha}
\xi_{\alpha\mu}
\mu_{L}
\tau_{L}
Z^{\prime}
\ell_{L\alpha}

(b)

Figure 36
:

to

. Photon lines are implicit. The index

.

In Fig.
we can see that the
Z^{\prime}
contribution
dominates the branching fraction in the range of large
x_{34}^{\psi}

motivated by
R_{D^{(*)}}
, leading to the predictions for
\mathcal{B}\left(\tau\rightarrow\mu\gamma\right)

being one/three orders of magnitude below the current experimental
limit depending on the value of
s_{23}^{e}
. We have also included
the bound projected by Belle II
[
255
]
,
which will partially test the parameter space. In the 4321 models
of
[
267
,
268
]
the
\mu-\tau
mixing
is unspecified, so only the blue
U_{1}
signal is predicted. For
non-fermiophobic models, this signal is largely enhanced via a chirality
flip involving the bottom quark in the loop
[
262
,
263
,
356
,
361
,
360
]
,
predicting a larger signal
\mathcal{B}\left(\tau\rightarrow\mu\gamma\right)\approx 10^{-8}
.
Instead, our
Z^{\prime}
signal lies below, offering the opportunity to
disentangle the twin Pati-Salam model from all alternative proposals.

\boldsymbol{B_{s}\rightarrow\tau\mu}
,
\boldsymbol{B\rightarrow K\tau\mu}

and
\boldsymbol{\tau\rightarrow\mu\phi}

(a)

The vector leptoquark
U_{1}
mediates tree-level contributions to
flavour-violating (semi) leptonic
B
-decays to (kaons), taus and
muons. The experimental bound for
B_{s}\rightarrow\tau\mu
was obtained
by LHCb
[
277
]
, while for
B\rightarrow K\tau\mu
experimental
bounds are only available for the decays
B^{+}\rightarrow K^{+}\tau\mu

[
278
]
. The process
\tau\rightarrow\mu\phi
receives
tree-level contributions from both
U_{1}
and
Z^{\prime}
. However,
\tau\rightarrow\mu\phi

turns out to be suppressed by the small effective couplings
\beta_{s\mu}\propto s_{25}^{Q}s_{25}^{L}

and
\xi_{ss}\propto\left(s_{25}^{Q}\right)^{2}
, so that we find

\mathcal{B}\left(\tau\rightarrow\mu\phi\right)\approx 10^{-9}
, roughly
two orders of magnitude below the current experimental bounds, and
just below the future sensitivity of Belle II
[
255
]
.

As can be seen in Fig.
,
B_{s}\rightarrow\tau^{+}\mu^{-}

implies the strongest constraint over the parameter space out of all
semileptonic LFV processes involving
\tau
leptons, followed by

B^{+}\rightarrow K^{+}\tau^{+}\mu^{-}
and
\tau\rightarrow\mu\phi
.
The present experimental bounds lead to mild constraints over the
parameter space compatible with
R_{D^{(*)}}
. As depicted in Fig.
,
the 2021 region for
B_{s}\rightarrow\tau^{+}\mu^{-}
was partially
within LHCb projected sensitivity, but the 2022 region will mostly
remain untested.

Table 16
:
Left:
Charge assignments under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
that allow

Right:
Benchmark parameters
which lead to a dilution
\epsilon<0.1
.

The LFV process
K_{L}\rightarrow\mu e
sets a strong bound
over all models featuring a vector leptoquark
U_{1}
coupled
to the first and second families. In our model, the contribution is
proportional to the couplings
\left|\beta_{de}\beta_{s\mu}^{*}\right|^{2}

(see the tree-level matching to the SMEFT in Section
21

and the EFT description of
K_{L}\rightarrow\mu e
in Section
13.9
).
The first family coupling
\beta_{de}
can be diluted via mixing
with vector-like fermions, which we parameterised via the effective
parameter
\epsilon
in Eq. (
559
), so that
\beta_{se}\approx s_{16}^{Q}s_{16}^{L}\epsilon
.

This can be done by adding an extra sixth-primed vector-like family transforming
in the same way as the sixth family under the twin Pati-Salam symmetry,
but discriminated by a flavour symmetry which we assume as
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

for simplicity (we could use the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
symmetry of the model as
well), which forbids mixing between the sixth-primed family and any
chiral family. Instead, mixing between the sixth and sixth-primed
fermion families is allowed via a twin Pati-Salam singlet charged
under the new
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, i.e.

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{mix}}=x_{66}\chi\psi^{\prime}_{6}\overline{\psi}_{6}+x^{\prime}_{66}\chi^{*}\psi{}_{6}\overline{\psi}^{\prime}_{6}\,+\mathrm{h.c.}

(599)

The mass terms of the sixth and sixth-primed fields are split via

\Omega_{15}
in the usual way,

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{mass}}=(M_{66}^{\psi}+\lambda_{15}^{66}T_{15}\Omega_{15})\psi_{6}\overline{\psi}_{6}+(M_{66^{\prime}}^{\psi}+\lambda_{15}^{66^{\prime}}T_{15}\Omega_{15})\psi^{\prime}_{6}\overline{\psi}^{\prime}_{6}+\mathrm{h.c.}

(600)

After
\Omega_{15}
and the singlet
\chi
develop VEVs, we obtain
the following mass matrices for quarks and leptons

\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{mass}}+\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{mix}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}lcc@{}}&Q_{6}&Q^{\prime}_{6}\\
\cline{2-3}\cr\left.\overline{Q}{}_{6}\right|&M_{66}^{Q}&x_{66}\left\langle\chi\right\rangle\\
\left.\overline{Q}^{\prime}_{6}\right|&x^{\prime}_{66}\left\langle\chi\right\rangle&M_{66^{\prime}}^{Q}\end{array}\right)+\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}lcc@{}}&L_{6}&L^{\prime}_{6}\\
\cline{2-3}\cr\left.\overline{L}{}_{6}\right|&M_{66}^{L}&x_{66}\left\langle\chi\right\rangle\\
\left.\overline{L}^{\prime}_{6}\right|&x^{\prime}_{66}\left\langle\chi\right\rangle&M_{66^{\prime}}^{L}\end{array}\right)+\mathrm{h.c.}\,,

(601)

where we have defined

M_{66}^{Q}=M_{66}^{\psi}+\frac{\lambda_{15}^{66}}{2\sqrt{6}}\left\langle\Omega_{15}\right\rangle\,,\quad M_{66}^{L}=M_{66}^{\psi}-3\frac{\lambda_{15}^{66}}{2\sqrt{6}}\left\langle\Omega_{15}\right\rangle\,,

(602)

M_{66^{\prime}}^{Q}=M_{66^{\prime}}^{\psi}+\frac{\lambda_{15}^{66^{\prime}}}{2\sqrt{6}}\left\langle\Omega_{15}\right\rangle\,,\quad M_{66^{\prime}}^{L}=M_{66^{\prime}}^{\psi}-3\frac{\lambda_{15}^{66^{\prime}}}{2\sqrt{6}}\left\langle\Omega_{15}\right\rangle\,.

(603)

The mass matrices in Eq. (
601
)
are diagonalised by different unitary transformations in the quark
and lepton sector,
V_{66^{\prime}}^{Q}
and
V_{66^{\prime}}^{L}
, in such a way
that the
U_{1}
couplings are given by

\mathcal{L}_{U_{1}}=\frac{g_{4}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}Q_{6}^{\dagger}&Q_{6}^{\dagger^{\prime}}\end{array}\right)\gamma_{\mu}V_{66^{\prime}}^{Q}\mathrm{diag}(1,1)V_{66^{\prime}}^{L\dagger}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}L_{6}\\
L^{\prime}_{6}\end{array}\right)U_{1}^{\mu}+\mathrm{h.c.}

(604)

If we define

V_{66^{\prime}}^{Q}V_{66^{\prime}}^{L\dagger}\equiv\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\cos\theta_{6}&\sin\theta_{6}\\
-\sin\theta_{6}&\cos\theta_{6}\end{array}\right)\,,

(605)

then the first family
U_{1}
coupling receives a suppression via

\cos\theta_{6}
as

\beta_{de}=s_{16}^{Q}s_{16}^{L}\cos\theta_{6}\,.

(606)

which is identified with the suppression parameter
\epsilon
in
Eq. (
559
),

\epsilon\equiv\cos\theta_{6}.

(607)

We can achieve values of
\cos\theta_{6}
smaller than 0.1 without
any aggressive tuning of the parameters, obtaining the mild suppression
desired for
K_{L}\rightarrow\mu e
as per Fig.
.
A suitable benchmark can be found in Table
16
.
Interestingly, this mechanism does not affect the
Z^{\prime}
and
g^{\prime}

interactions, as the unitary matrices
V_{66^{\prime}}^{Q}
and
V_{66^{\prime}}^{L}

cancel in neutral currents. This allows the GIM-like protection from
1-2 FCNCs to remain in place for both the quark and lepton sectors
via
s_{16}^{Q}=s_{25}^{Q}
and
s_{16}^{L}=s_{25}^{L}
, without
entering in conflict with
K_{L}\rightarrow\mu e
nor with
B
-physics.

(b)

)) as a function of

))

.

for

we can see that for the 2022 case, some

K_{L}\rightarrow\mu e

without the need of diluting the coupling. Instead, for the benchmark
values BP1 and BP2, a mild suppression is required. This signal is a direct consequence
of the underlying twin Pati-Salam symmetry and the GIM-like mechanism, which
lead to quasi-degenerate mixing angles
s_{16}^{Q}\approx s_{16}^{L}

that are equal to their 25 counterparts, and as a consequence
\beta_{de}\neq 0
.
Therefore, it is not present in other 4321 models
[
267
,

,

,

,
356
]
.

24.5
Tests of universality in leptonic tau decays

NP contributions to
R_{D^{(*)}}
commonly involve large couplings
to tau leptons, which can have an important effect over LFU ratios
originated from tau decays. Such tests are constructed by performing
ratios of the partial widths of the tau lepton decaying to lighter leptons
and/or hadrons. We find all ratios in our model to be well approximated
by (see the tree-level matching to the SMEFT in Section
21

and the EFT description of
\tau
LFU ratios in Section
13.10
),

\left(\frac{g_{\tau}}{g_{\mu,e}}\right)_{\ell+\pi+K}\approx 1-0.079C_{U}\left|\beta_{b\tau}\right|^{2}\,,

(608)

where
\beta_{b\tau}\approx\cos\theta_{LQ}
assuming maximal 3-4
mixing. Therefore, it can be seen as a constraint over the
\beta_{b\tau}

coupling, and hence is not directly related to
R_{K^{(*)}}
so we
do not plot two bands here. The high-precision measurements of these
effective ratios only allow for per mil modifications, see the HFLAV
average
[
80
]
in Table
14
. As
depicted in Fig.
, this constraint sets
the lower bound
M_{U_{1}}\gtrsim 2.2\,\mathrm{TeV}
for
\sin\theta_{LQ}=1/\sqrt{2}

and
g_{4}=3.5
. This bound becomes more restrictive for
\cos\theta_{LQ}\approx 1
,
or equivalently
\beta_{b\tau}\approx 1
, for which we find
M_{U_{1}}\gtrsim 3.3\,\mathrm{TeV}

if
g_{4}=3.5
and
M_{U_{1}}\gtrsim 2.9\,\mathrm{TeV}
if
g_{4}=3
.
The latter case, more constrained by data, is predicted by non-fermiophobic
4321 models such as
\mathrm{PS^{3}}
and variants
[
262
,
263
,
356
,
115
,
170
]
.

Signals in rare
B
-decays

\boldsymbol{B_{s}\rightarrow\tau\tau}
and
\boldsymbol{B\rightarrow K\tau\tau}

(a)

(b)

As anticipated in Section
24.2
,
the enhancement of
R_{D^{(*)}}
via the
U_{1}
leptoquark is correlated
to an enhancement of
b\rightarrow s\tau\tau
via
SU(2)_{L}
invariance
of the
U_{1}
couplings to fermions. The respective branching fractions
are of order
10^{-7}
in the SM and mild upper bounds have been
obtained by LHCb
[

]
and BaBar
[

]
,

for a further

, we plot the branching fractions as a

x_{34}^{\psi}
, while
x_{25}^{\psi}
is varied in
the ranges compatible with 2021 and 2022
R_{K^{(*)}}
, respectively.
We find that the predictions are far below the current bounds, however
they lie closer to the expected future bounds from LHCb and Belle
II data
[
253
,
255
]
. This prediction is
different in non-fermiophobic 4321 models
[
262
,
263
,
356
]
,
where these contributions are chirally enhanced due to the presence of scalar operators, and all the parameter space
is expected to be tested in the
B^{+}\rightarrow K^{+}\tau^{+}\tau^{-}

process by Belle II, see the full discussion in Section
13.6
.

The lifetime ratio
\tau_{B_{s}}/\tau_{B_{d}}
introduced in Section
13.6

can potentially provide significant direct bounds over
\mathcal{B}\left(B_{s}\rightarrow\tau^{+}\tau^{-}\right)

in the future
[
4
]
, which can discriminate as well between the twin Pati-Salam
model and non-fermiophobic 4321 models, see Fig.
12
.

\boldsymbol{B\rightarrow K\nu\nu}

The
U_{1}
leptoquark does not contribute at tree-level to
b\rightarrow s\nu\nu

transitions, and the tree-level exchange of the
Z^{\prime}
is suppressed
due to the down-aligned flavour structure of the model. However, loop-level
corrections can lead to an important enhancement of the channel
B\rightarrow K\nu_{\tau}\bar{\nu}_{\tau}

[
263
]
. We parameterise corrections to the SM branching
fraction as in Eq. (
236
), where the EFT and the Wilson
coefficients are defined in Section
13.7
.

We only obtain sizable contributions in the
\tau\tau
channel due
to the flavour structure of the model, and we split the NP effects
into
Z^{\prime}
and
U_{1}
contributions as follows,

C_{\nu,\mathrm{NP}}^{\tau\tau}=-\frac{1}{V_{tb}V_{ts}^{*}}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{4G_{F}}\left(C_{\nu,Z^{\prime}}^{\tau\tau}+C_{\nu,U_{1}}^{\tau\tau}\right)\,.

(609)

The
U_{1}
contribution at NLO accuracy reads
[
360
]

C_{\nu,U_{1}}^{\tau\tau}\approx C_{\nu,U_{1}}^{\mathrm{RGE}}+\frac{g_{4}^{4}}{32\pi^{2}M^{2}_{U_{1}}}\sum_{\alpha,j}\left(\beta_{s\alpha}^{*}\beta_{b\alpha}\right)\left(\beta_{j\nu_{\tau}}\right)^{2}F(x_{\alpha},x_{j})\,,

(610)

where the second term arises from the semileptonic box diagram in
Fig.
, and the first term encodes the
RGE-induced contribution from the tree-level leptoquark-mediated operator

[C_{ed}^{V,LL}]^{\tau\tau 23}
, computed with
DsixTools 2.1

[
174
]

as

s_{L}
\beta_{s\alpha}
\beta_{j\nu_{\tau}}^{*}
\nu_{\tau}
\beta_{b\alpha}^{*}
\beta_{j\nu_{\tau}}
b_{L}
\nu_{\tau}
\ell_{L\alpha}
U_{1}
u_{Lj}
U_{1}

(a)

s_{L}
b_{L}
\nu_{\tau}
\nu_{\tau}
L_{5}
U_{1}
h_{U}
Z^{\prime}

(b)

Figure 40
:
Box and penguin diagrams contributing to
B\rightarrow K\nu\nu
.
The index
\alpha
runs for all charged leptons including vector-like,
i.e.
\ell_{L\alpha}=\left(\mu_{L},\tau_{L},E_{L4},E_{L5}\right)
,
and the index

runs for all up-type quarks, including vector-like

u_{Lj}=\left(c_{L},t_{L},U_{L4},U_{L5}\right)
. See more details
in the main text.

C_{\nu,U_{1}}^{\mathrm{RGE}}=0.047\frac{g_{4}^{2}}{2M_{U_{1}}^{2}}\beta_{b\tau}\beta_{s\tau}\,.

(611)

The
Z^{\prime}
contribution to NLO accuracy reads

\displaystyle C_{\nu,Z^{\prime}}^{\tau\tau}\approx\frac{3g_{4}^{2}}{2M_{Z^{\prime}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\left[\xi_{bs}\xi_{\nu_{\tau}\nu_{\tau}}\left(1+\frac{3}{2}\frac{g_{4}^{2}}{16\pi^{2}}\xi_{\nu_{\tau}\nu_{\tau}}^{2}\right)\right.

(612)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{g_{4}^{2}}{16\pi^{2}}\beta_{sE_{5}}^{*}\beta_{bE_{5}}\xi_{\nu_{\tau}\nu_{\tau}}G_{\Delta Q=1}(x_{E_{5}},x_{Z^{\prime}},x_{R})\right]\,,

where
x_{E_{5}}\equiv(M_{5}^{L}/M_{U_{1}})^{2}
,
x_{Z^{\prime}}\equiv M_{Z^{\prime}}^{2}/M_{U_{1}}^{2}

and
x_{R}\equiv M_{R}^{2}/M_{U_{1}}^{2}
with
M_{R}
being a scale
associated to the radial mode
h_{U}(\mathbf{3,1},2/3)
arising from

\phi_{3,1}
. The first term in Eq. (
612
) corresponds
to the tree-level contribution plus a 1-loop
Z^{\prime}
correction to the
leptonic vertex. The coupling
\xi_{bs}
is suppressed by the small
down mixing angle
\theta_{23}^{d}\approx 0.001
, leading to per cent
corrections to
\mathcal{B}(B\rightarrow K^{(*)}\nu\bar{\nu})
. The
second term in Eq. (
612
) corresponds to a 1-loop
correction to the flavour-violating
Z^{\prime}
vertex, with
U_{1}
, the
fifth vector-like lepton
E_{5}
and
h_{U}
running in the loop,
see Fig.
. The loop function is given
by
[
263
,
360
]

G_{\Delta Q=1}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\approx\frac{5}{4}x_{1}+\frac{x_{1}}{2}\left(x_{2}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\ln x_{3}-\frac{5}{2}\right)\,.

(613)

In the twin Pati-Salam framework, we expect extra radial modes associated
to
\overline{\phi}_{3,1}
and
\overline{\phi^{\prime}}_{3,1}
, however they only
couple to right-handed chiral fermions and hence they cannot contribute to
C_{\nu,\mathrm{NP}}^{\tau\tau}

which involves only left-handed chiral fermions.

Both 1-loop contributions are dominated by the fifth vector-like charged lepton
and grow with its bare mass,
M_{5}^{L}
. This way, the overall contribution
to
B\rightarrow K\nu\nu
can be sizable, yielding up to
\mathcal{O}(1)

corrections with respect to the SM value, as depicted in Fig.
41
.

Figure 41
:
\delta\mathcal{B}(B\rightarrow K^{(*)}\nu\bar{\nu})
(Eq. (
236
))
as a function of the 5th family vector-like mass term.

is varied in the range
x_{25}^{\psi}=[0.3,\,0.35]
(
[0.4,\,0.45]
)
preferred by
R_{K^{(*)}}^{2022}
(

), obtaining

\Delta M_{s}

bound, see Eq. (
214
). The grey region is excluded
by current experimental measurements, the dashed line indicates the
projected future bound.

For low
M_{5}^{L}
, the enhancement of
\delta\mathcal{B}(B\rightarrow K^{(*)}\nu\bar{\nu})

corresponds mostly to
C_{\nu,U}^{\mathrm{RGE}}
. For large
M_{5}^{L}
,
however, we have seen that stringent constraints from
B_{s}-\bar{B}_{s}

meson mixing play an important role, see Section
24.3
.
This constraint is depicted as the hatched region in Fig.
41
,
correlating
B\rightarrow K\nu\nu
and
\Delta M_{s}
, a feature
which has not been highlighted in previous analyses. In particular,

\Delta M_{s}
rules out the region where
\delta\mathcal{B}(B\rightarrow K^{(*)}\nu\bar{\nu})

can reach values close to current experimental limits, making impossible to address the
2.8\sigma
anomaly in
\mathcal{B}(B^{+}\rightarrow K^{+}\nu\bar{\nu})
suggested by the recent Belle II data
[
81
]
. Nevertheless,
the Belle II collaboration is expected to measure
\mathcal{B}(B\rightarrow K^{(*)}\nu\bar{\nu})

up to 10% of the SM value
[
255
]
, hence testing
all the parameter space of our model.

Our signal of
B\rightarrow K^{(*)}\nu\bar{\nu}
also offers a great
opportunity to disentangle our twin Pati-Salam framework from non-fermiophobic
4321 models and from the
\mathrm{PS}^{3}
model
[
262
,
263
,
356
,
115
]
,
as they predict a much smaller signal (see Fig. 4.4 of
[
263
]

and compare their purple region with our Fig.
41
).

24.7
Perturbativity

The explanation of the
R_{D^{(*)}}
anomaly requires large mixing
angles
s_{34}^{Q}
and
s_{34}^{L}
, which translate into a sizeable
Yukawa coupling
x_{34}^{\psi}
, thus pushing the model close to
the boundary of the perturbative domain. Perturbativity is a serious
constraint over our model, since we need the low-energy 4321 theory
to remain perturbative until the high scale of the twin Pati-Salam
symmetry. When assessing the issue of perturbativity, two conditions
must be satisfied:

•

Firstly, the low-energy observables must be calculable in perturbation
theory. For Yukawa couplings, we consider the bound
x_{34}^{\psi}<\sqrt{4\pi}
.
Regarding the gauge coupling
g_{4}
, standard perturbativity criteria
imposes the beta function criterion
[
362
]

\left|\beta_{g_{4}}/g_{4}\right|<1
,
which yields
g_{4}<4\pi\sqrt{3}/\sqrt{28}\approx 4.11
.

•

Secondly, the couplings must remain perturbative up to the energy
scale of the UV completion, i.e. we have to check that the couplings
of the model do not face a Landau pole below the energy scale of the
second PS breaking, namely
\mu\approx 1\,\mathrm{PeV}
.

The phenomenologically convenient choice of large
g_{4}
is not
a problem for the extrapolation in the UV, thanks to the asymptotic
freedom of the
SU(4)
gauge factor (see Fig.
42
).
To investigate the running of the most problematic Yukawa
x_{34}^{\psi}
,
we use the 1-loop renormalisation group equations of the
4321 model. For the gauge coupling beta functions
\beta_{g_{i}}=(dg_{i}/d\mu)/\mu

we have
[
268
]

\left(4\pi\right)^{2}\beta_{g_{1}}=\frac{131}{18}g_{1}^{3}\,,\quad\left(4\pi\right)^{2}\beta_{g_{2}}=\left(-\frac{19}{6}+\frac{8n_{\Psi}}{3}\right)g_{2}^{3}\,,

(614)

\left(4\pi\right)^{2}\beta_{g_{3}}=-\frac{19}{3}g_{3}^{3}\,,\quad\left(4\pi\right)^{2}\beta_{g_{4}}=\left(-\frac{40}{3}+\frac{4n_{\Psi}}{3}\right)g_{4}^{3}\,,

(615)

where
n_{\Psi}=3
is the number of vector-like fermion families.
The Pati-Salam universality of the Yukawas
x_{i\alpha}^{\psi}
is
broken by RGE effects which we quantify through the equations

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left(4\pi\right)^{2}\beta_{x_{Q}}&=\frac{7}{2}x_{Q}x_{Q}^{\dagger}x_{Q}+\frac{1}{2}x_{Q}x_{L}^{\dagger}x_{L}+\frac{15}{8}x_{Q}\lambda_{15}\lambda_{15}^{\dagger}+2\mathrm{Tr}\left(x_{Q}x_{Q}^{\dagger}\right)x_{Q}\\
{}&-\frac{1}{12}g_{1}^{2}x_{Q}-\frac{9}{2}g_{2}^{2}x_{Q}-4g_{3}^{2}x_{Q}-\frac{45}{8}g_{4}^{2}x_{Q}\,,\end{aligned}

(616)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left(4\pi\right)^{2}\beta_{x_{L}}&=\frac{5}{2}x_{L}x_{L}^{\dagger}x_{L}+\frac{3}{2}x_{L}x_{Q}^{\dagger}x_{Q}+\frac{15}{8}x_{L}\lambda_{15}\lambda_{15}^{\dagger}+2\mathrm{Tr}\left(x_{L}x_{L}^{\dagger}\right)x_{L}\\
{}&-\frac{3}{4}g_{1}^{2}x_{L}-\frac{9}{2}g_{2}^{2}x_{L}-\frac{45}{8}g_{4}^{2}x_{L}\,,\end{aligned}

(617)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left(4\pi\right)^{2}\beta_{\lambda_{15}}&=\frac{21}{4}\lambda_{15}\lambda_{15}\lambda_{15}^{\dagger}+\frac{3}{2}\lambda_{15}x_{Q}^{\dagger}x_{Q}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{15}x_{L}^{\dagger}x_{L}+4\mathrm{Tr}\left(\lambda_{15}\lambda_{15}^{\dagger}\right)\lambda_{15}\\
{}&-\frac{9}{2}g_{2}^{2}\lambda_{15}-\frac{45}{4}g_{4}^{2}\lambda_{15}\,,\end{aligned}

(618)

where any contributions from the Yukawas of the personal Higgs,
y_{i\alpha}^{\psi}
,
are negligible as they are all 1 or smaller.

Figure 42
:
RGE of the gauge couplings in our benchmark scenario from the TeV
scale to the scale of the twin Pati-Salam symmetry
\mu\sim 1\,\mathrm{PeV}
.

The running of the effective Yukawa couplings is protected, as the
top Yukawa is order 1 and all of the others are smaller, SM-like (see
the discussion in Section
23.2
). This
feature is different from
[
268
]
which was not a
theory of flavour, causing the top mass to be accidentally suppressed
by the equivalent of
c_{34}^{Q}
in our model, hence requiring a
large, non-perturbative top Yukawa to preserve the top mass. Instead,
in our model the effective top Yukawa arises proportional to the maximal
angle
s_{34}^{Q}
, rendering the top Yukawa natural and perturbative.
The matrices of couplings
x_{Q,L}
and
\lambda_{15}
are defined
as (assuming small
x_{35}^{\psi}
as discussed in Section
24.3
)

x_{\psi}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}x_{16}^{\psi}&0&0\\
0&x_{25}^{\psi}&0\\
0&0&x_{34}^{\psi}\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\lambda_{15}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\lambda_{15}^{6}&0&0\\
0&\lambda_{15}^{5}&0\\
0&0&\lambda_{15}^{4}\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\psi=Q,\,L\,.

(619)

The Yukawas
x_{25}^{\psi}
and
x_{16}^{\psi}
are not dangerous
as they are order 1 of smaller. The problematic Yukawa is
x_{34}^{\psi}
,
which is required to be large in order to address
R_{D^{(*)}}
,
and it is also connected with the physical mass of the fourth family lepton
as per Eq. (
542
). Large
\lambda_{15}^{5}
is also
required to obtain a large splitting of vector-like masses, which provides
a large
\theta_{LQ}
as required by
R_{D^{(*)}}
.

(a)

(b)

Figure 43
:
RGE of the fundamental Yukawa couplings in our benchmark scenario
(Table
15
) from the TeV scale to the scale of the twin
Pati-Salam symmetry
\mu\sim 1\,\mathrm{PeV}
. The left panel shows
the
x_{i\alpha}^{\psi}
Yukawas which lead to the mixing between
SM fermions and vector-like partners. The right panel shows the
\lambda_{15}

Yukawas which split the vector-like masses of quarks and leptons.

Fig.
43
shows that the Yukawas of
our benchmark scenario remain perturbative up to the high energy scale

\mu\approx 1\,\mathrm{PeV}
, thanks to the choice of a large
g_{4}=3.5
.
However, we have checked that the Landau pole is hit when
x_{34}^{\psi}>2.5
,
hence this region should be considered as disfavoured by the perturbativity
criteria.

The small RGE effects that break the Pati-Salam universality of the Yukawa
couplings are below 8% in any case, hence the universality of the
couplings is preserved at the TeV scale to good approximation.

24.8
High-
p_{T}
signatures

General 4321 models predict a plethora of high-
p_{T}
signatures
involving the heavy gauge bosons and at least one family of vector-like
fermions, requiring dedicated analyses such as those in
[
268
,
363
,
263
]
.
In particular, our model predicts a similar high-
p_{T}
phenomenology
as that of
[
268
]
, which also considers effective

U_{1}
couplings via mixing with three families of vector-like fermions.
However, the bounds obtained in the high-
p_{T}
analysis of
[
268
]

are mostly outdated. Moreover, certain differences arise due to the
underlying twin Pati-Salam symmetry in our model, plus the different
implementation of the scalar sector and VEV structure. In contrast,
the most recent high-
p_{T}
studies of the 4321 model assume a non-fermiophobic
framework
[
263
,
363
]
, where the heavy
gauge bosons have large couplings to right-handed third family fermions.
In this manner, some of the bounds in
[
263
,
363
]

are overestimated for our model. This motivates a dedicated high-
p_{T}

analysis of the twin Pati-Salam model.
